Actually N=O attached to nitrogen. That's a nitrosamine group (R2N-NO). Nitrosamines are known carcinogens, mutagenic. This is a nitrosamine derivative; many nitrosamines are mutagenic. Example: N-nitrosodimethylamine is mutagenic. So likely mutagenic. The similar molecules list: top 3 similar with label (B) include nitroso group. So seems mutagenic. The label (A) group includes one with nitroso also? In top 3 similar for A, reference 1 has nitroso but also secondary hydroxyl etc. But still has nitroso. So classification is ambiguous. But nitrosamine is strong mutagen. Many structural alerts: Nitro group (N-O) attached to nitrogen. So I lean B.

Also from properties: no basic or acidic sites, cationic? The amine might be tertiary neutral; but nitrosation leads to N-nitrosation? In fact, N-nitrosamines are neutral, can cause DNA alkylation. Ames test positive typically.

Thus answer B.
